# 🗺️ The Combinatorics & Graph Theory Atlas

*A self-contained, growing book: the canon of solved problems in combinatorics & graph theory, grouped by solution pattern, with every standard method of solving each problem.*

**Purpose.** One honest note: the published literature contains over a hundred thousand problems and papers, so "all problems" is not a book anyone can hold. What this Atlas captures instead is the **canon** — the load-bearing solved problems that every researcher must know — plus a complete taxonomy of the **solution patterns** (the repeatable moves) that solve them. New problems and proofs are added session by session; this file is the map and the progress report, so no session ever loses the thread.

**How to use.** Read a chapter canvas like a book: each chapter opens with its *pattern table*, then works problems. Every problem is presented in the same template:

> **Problem** → **Method 1…k** (independent, complete solutions) → **What to learn** (which pattern won, and when to reuse it).

---

## 1 · The Master Pattern Index

The central idea: problems are grouped not by topic but by the *move that solves them*. Across all chapters, the recurring patterns are:

| ID | Pattern | One-line description | Home chapters |
|----|---------|----------------------|---------------|
| P1 | **Bijection** | Count A by matching it 1-to-1 with a set B you can count | 1, 2 |
| P2 | **Double counting** | Count the same set two ways; equate | 1, 2, 3 |
| P3 | **Inclusion–exclusion** | Add, subtract overlaps back | 1 |
| P4 | **Recurrence + induction** | Relate case n to smaller cases | 1, 2, 3 |
| P5 | **Generating function** | Turn a sequence into a function; let algebra work | 1 |
| P6 | **Complement / reflection** | Count the complement or reflect the bad objects | 1 |
| P7 | **Parity & handshaking** | Degree-sum is even; ends of arguments | 2 |
| P8 | **Longest-path / extremal** | Take an extremal object; its structure forces a cycle, leaf, etc. | 2, 3 |
| P9 | **Pigeonhole** | n+1 objects, n boxes — and the lower the contest level, the more it carries the whole proof (the survey's level gradient: Putnam small-step, USAMO obstruction, IMO whole-proof engine) | 3 |
| P10 | **Averaging / expectation** | Some object beats the average | 3, 4 |
| P11 | **Explicit construction** | Beat the bound by *building* the object | 3 |
| P12 | **Random construction** | Prove existence by sampling | 4 |
| P13 | **Union bound** | If failure probabilities sum below 1, success exists | 4 |
| P14 | **Alteration** | Random object + pruning | 4 |
| P15 | **Induction on vertices/edges** | Delete a vertex, extend the solution | 2, 3 |
| P16 | **Alternating paths / augmentation** | Improve a partial solution along a path | 5 |
| P17 | **Duality (min–max)** | Two problems are the same number | 5 |
| P18 | **Algorithmic proof** | The proof *is* a procedure; termination = theorem | 2, 5 |
| P19 | **Rank / linear algebra** | Encode sets as vectors; dimension bounds | 6 |
| P20 | **Weight shifting / symmetrization** | Push weight to the extremal configuration | 3, 6 |
| P21 | **Spectral / Rayleigh** | Eigenvalues as averaging devices; operator norm on 1⊥ | 6 |
| P22 | **Polynomial method** | Encode sets as polynomials; dimension counting | 6 |
| P23 | **Compression / shifting** | Replace sets by canonical "heavier" ones without losing the property | 7 |
| P24 | **Shadow pricing (Kruskal–Katona)** | Price a family by its own real parameter x: a k-family of size C(x, k) forces every j-shadow to size ≥ C(x, j) — equality only at integer x and the complete families | 7, 9 |
| P25 | **Reduction / encoding** | Translate one problem into another’s language; the target structure carries the proof or the hardness | 3, 10, 11 |
| P26 | **Compactness (finite ↔ infinite)** | Prove the infinite statement, pull the finite one back via König’s lemma — or push the finite one to a limit | 2, 10 |
| P27 | **Correlation** | Up-sets lean together: P(∩ good) ≥ ∏P(good) is a floor — FKG beyond product measure | 13 |
| P28 | **Concentration** | Bounded exposure cannot stray far from its mean — Azuma, Talagrand | 13 |
| P29 | **Entropy counting** | Count by description cost: H ≤ log support + the chain rule | 13 |
| P30 | **Regularity partitioning** | Every large graph is a bounded quasirandom blow-up; refine until the noise is uniform | 14 |
| P31 | **Counting–removal** | Regular structure multiplies densities; few copies ⟹ few deletions | 14 |
| P32 | **Stability** | Near-extremal objects sit near the extremal configuration, not merely near its count | 14 |
| P33 | **Density increment** | If the structure you want is nowhere, refine: some cell gains density | 14 |
| P34 | **Minor chasing** | Build a shadow-clique from disjoint connected branch sets; deletion and contraction are the moves | 15 |
| P35 | **Dynamic programming on structure** | Tree decompositions turn global problems into bag-local recurrences | 15 |
| P36 | **Discharging** | Give every local object a charge; Euler’s formula fixes the total; some local configuration must go into deficit | 15 |
| P37 | **Duality** | Two theories, one object: plane duals exchange vertex-coloring with edge-flowing | 15 |
| P38 | **Replication** | Duplicate a vertex to force structure; twins inherit and transmit perfection | 15 |
| P39 | **Symmetry averaging** | To count what symmetry identifies, average fixed points over the group; the cycle index prices each symmetry’s orbit structure | 16 |
| P40 | **Möbius inversion** | Invert summatory functions on a poset: the μ function strips double-counted weight back to the primitive layer | 16 |
| P41 | **Double-counting & divisibility** | Integrality of the replication numbers r and λ is a design’s birth certificate: non-integral parameters forbid existence before any search | 16 |
| P42 | **Words-as-designs** | A code’s weight classes can carry Steiner structure: Assmus–Mattson turns distance into t-designs (Golay → Witt) | 16 |
| P43 | **Semi-random nibble** | Reveal a tiny random slice of the structure at a time and track one parameter through every nibble: the tracked quantity moves by honest conditional expectation each round, so the process converges on the full structure (Rödl's nibble; Pippenger–Spencer) | 17 |
| P44 | **Threshold / window** | The window is the theorem: a random structure's property turns on inside a scaling window whose position and width carry the information (Erdős–Rényi evolution; Friedgut–Kalai sharpness; the Kahn–Kalai expectation threshold, proved Park–Pham) | 18 |
| P45 | **Local lemma** | Rare, sparsely dependent bad events can be simultaneously avoided: certify survival seat by seat — p_i ≤ x_i·∏_{j∈Γ(i)}(1−x_j) — when no union bound can total them (Erdős–Lovász 1975; Spencer's asymmetric form; the lopsided variant; Moser–Tardos's resampling algorithm; Shearer's boundary) | 19 |

*P27–P29 were proposed at Chapter 13's ship (session 51, shipped as Ch. 14), P30–P33 at Chapter 14's ship (session 52, shipped as Ch. 15), and P34–P38 at Chapter 15's ship (session 53, shipped as Ch. 16), and P39–P42 at Chapter 16's ship (session 54, shipped as Ch. 17), per the session-50 scope ("new P-ID proposals per chapter at ship"); all sixteen remain forward-registry entries — the reverse index’s regeneration (session 55) confirmed that no problem or hint names any of them yet, and their rows in the reverse index record this honestly. (Dated, session 83: P41 is the first of the sixteen to exit — Ch. 20's Exercise 6 names divisibility's cameo in its hint; the row above records it. P43/P44/P45 exited earlier by the home-chapter route, which is different — those are their chapters' own patterns.)*

*P43 and P44 enter by a different route: proposed by the paper-pattern survey's chunk-15 synthesis (papers/ANALYSIS.md in the cgt-atlas-backup repo, commit a8b32dcb — the survey of 133 papers in 14 batches) and adopted into the registry at user direction (session 80). P43 **semi-random nibble** with parameter tracking was load-bearing in five survey batches (the Rödl 1985 nibble — the Erdős–Hanani conjecture; the Pippenger–Spencer 1989 formalization; Kahn's application; with Ajtai–Komlós–Szemerédi and Kim as ancestors; Keevash 2014 as the downstream design-theory application); P44 **threshold/window** fired in four (the Erdős–Rényi evolution; Friedgut–Kalai sharp thresholds; the Kahn–Kalai expectation-threshold conjecture proved by Park–Pham 2022) and is a phenomenon-pattern in the P26 manner — the window is the theorem. Both were forward-registry entries until their home chapters shipped — Ch. 17 (session 81) for P43, Ch. 18 (session 82) for P44; both are now pinned home with genuine-use reverse-index entries. The same synthesis's four face findings were installed in their home chapters' pattern cards the same session, recorded here for the registry: the sign-reversing involution is P1's dynamic face (Gessel–Viennot; Ch. 1); the monovariant is P4's termination face (Ch. 1); slice rank is the face of P19/P22 (noted in Ch. 6, home Ch. 14's cap-set lecture); transference/dense-model is the face of P30/P31 (Ch. 14's Green–Tao flagship). The synthesis's fifth and last face finding was installed at session 83 (chunk 83e): graphical Brascamp–Lieb is P10's functional-analysis face (Ch. 4's Trick Bag — the Hölder lineage the survey watched grow from N2's verification step to N8's whole engine, the reverse-Sidorenko Inventiones landing of Sah–Sawhney–Stoner–Zhao 2020); the same chunk executed the synthesis's instruction for faces spanning SEVERAL patterns — the one-face-two-entries detailed note, both patterns named, no double credit — on Ch. 6's slice-rank row (P19/P22) and Ch. 14's transference row (P30/P31), each with a dated appendix record. The synthesis's usage-evidence notes (its §3–§4 honest negatives) were adopted at session 83 (chunk 83f), each installed in its pattern's home-chapter card with a dated appendix record: P6 complement/reflection has ZERO firings in 133 papers (the only textbook-canon pattern with none — Ch. 1 presents it as textbook canon, not field-visible practice); P20 weight shifting is genuinely under-used (the symmetrization work the classics do is P23 shifting or P10 averaging — Katona's circle method is P10 partial work; noted in Ch. 3 and Ch. 6); P27 correlation is fully silent in the corpus (entry open — Ch. 13); P35 DP-on-structure has no genuine firing (one standard-tool partial touch — entry open, Ch. 15); P36 discharging's silence is a corpus-SAMPLING gap, not field-abandonment (the planar-map discharging tradition was never sampled — entry open, Ch. 15).*

*The same survey's contest-census extension (chunk 29 — the Putnam/IMO/USAMO/Kurschák/shortlist canons, every problem graded pattern-side) contributed four structural findings, adopted at session 83 (chunk 83g) as learning/practice aids and installed in Ch. 12, the Olympiad Bridge (the honest-census section + a dated appendix record): the reading-order hint — the pre-doctoral canon is exactly P25 (reduction/encoding), P4 (recurrence + induction), P1 (bijection), P9 (pigeonhole), P10 (averaging), triple-confirmed from the youth-paper side, the courseware side, and the Putnam side, with P3's research debut fired by a middle-school team (the last never-fired counting pattern, first fired by children) and the honest boundary P5/P16/P23/P30–P31; the P9 level gradient — implicit small-step at the Putnam level (2025 B4), decisive full obstruction at the USAMO level (2024 P4, USAJMO 2024 P4), route-pinned whole-proof mechanism at the IMO level (2024 P3), the lower the level the more the pigeonhole carries (P9's row above); the genre-split pedagogy note — contest combinatorics is at least three traditions (enumeration-heavy Putnam, construction-heavy USAMO with P11 ×9, process/game-heavy IMO, the classical construct-and-obstruct Kurschák closing the set with games absent), so the contest-side reading order is tradition-dependent; and the obstruction-halves coverage-gap cluster — the obstruction halves of contest extremal problems live disproportionately outside this index (modular-residue/CRT obstructions, the pairing and blocking strategies, chip-firing, the max-plus generators), with partial homes at Ch. 20's strategy/adaptive-adversary named method and Ch. 21's k-adic digit engine. Dated extension (session 84, 2026-10-15): the survey's chunk-30 upgrade passes upgraded two of the datums behind these findings — the 30a Bajnok-report pass route-pinned the 2022 P1, 2022 P6 and 2023 P5 IMO exam rows (with the 2024 P3/P5 double confirmations), moving the IMO exam route coverage from 2/8 to 5/8, and the 30d teaching pass completed the MIT 6.042J courseware census at 12 of 12 sets (ps4 pinned via the instructor's official CSAIL directory; P4/P1 genuine, one NO-P-ID state-machine row) — so the route-coverage honesty gradient and the courseware-side reading-order confirmation above now rest on the upgraded record (the dated extension note lives in Ch. 12's appendix); the remaining deferrals stay honestly open and registered in papers/STATE.md (the 2022 USAMO reports, the Kurschák finals, the 62nd/66th IMO reports, the 2026 exam and shortlist, the T5 mining).*

*P45 enters by the same survey route, one wave later: the survey record's pending list proposed chapters for its three borderline candidates; sessions 80–82 had already delivered P43's and P44's homes (Ch. 17, Ch. 18), and the open question — the local lemma as its own entry, or a strengthening of P13 — was resolved at user direction (session 83): **P45, its own pattern**, home Ch. 19 The Lovász Local Lemma (the symmetric form; Spencer's general asymmetric form with the seat calculus and printed floors; the lopsided variant of Erdős–Spencer 1991; the Moser–Tardos resampling algorithm as the constructive face; Shearer's boundary honestly stated — μ(P_m, 1/4) = (m+2)/2^{m+1} keeps the finite paths marginally inside). The chapter's appendix records the survey's usage evidence honestly: the A5/A7/A9 in-corpus firings (single-batch), the B9 Johansson finding — never journal-published, exposition-level via Molloy–Reed, with Molloy 2019 as the pinned descendant — and the entropy-compression candidate (CAND-A4) that died in-corpus, taught inside Ch. 19 with its external lineage (P29 the counting parent; Molloy 2019 the sharpening). Ch. 19 shipped at session 83 with its six lab panels verified on the documented engine and its receipts campaign recorded honestly (the p-slip, the one-index slip, two re-run panels, and the meter's corrected attribution).*


### Reverse pattern index — pattern → the problems that use it

The forward table above maps patterns to home chapters; this one maps each pattern to the *problems that announce it*. Scope, honestly: a problem appears under a pattern exactly when its own hint names that pattern — generated machine-side, never transcribed by hand. First built session 42 over Ch. 1–9 (after the C1 backfill made pattern-ID hints an atlas-wide convention); regenerated session 55 over ALL chapters, same rules, three sources recorded honestly: (i) Ch. 1–11 — the graded exercises whose hints or statement parentheticals name a P-ID; (ii) Ch. 12 — no Exercises section by design, so the chapter’s own per-problem pattern map is the source (the thirty olympiad problems’ patterns, designated by the chapter rather than named by hints; its P26 appearance is flavor-only, so noted on the row); (iii) Ch. 17 — the Lean companion (renumbered 13 → 17 at session 56), no problems, nothing to index. Ch. 13–16’s exercises carry no pattern-ID hints by design — machine-verified at this build, every item’s text checked — so Volume III contributes no rows, and its new P-IDs (P27–P42) stand as forward-registry entries. Census at this build: 219 numbered exercises in Ch. 1–11 (175 graded ●, 31 ◆ pattern-unknown, 13 numbered ◐; the unnumbered explorations of the early chapters sit outside the numbering), of which 106 — every one a graded ● — carry pattern-ID hints (139 pattern mentions); Ch. 12’s map adds 45 problem–pattern pairs over 14 patterns; Ch. 13–16 add 72 further numbered items (48 graded, 12 ◆, 12 ◐), none carrying P-IDs. Problems naming several patterns appear under each. The ◆ problems are hint-free by design (diagnosing the pattern is the assignment), and ◐ explorations’ speculative pattern-naming questions do not count — Ch. 10’s ex. 18 names P11/P12/P25 as candidates in a question, excluded per the session-42 convention. P20 and P24 carry no named problems yet — recorded in their rows, not smoothed over; P17, empty at session 42, now fires in Ch. 10–12. Ch. 6’s P19′ variant (independence via dot products) is noted on P19’s row. One prose discrepancy recorded at the session-55 build without editing the chapter — Ch. 12’s lore row said thirteen patterns fired and the other thirteen never, over a never-fired enumeration of twelve — was repaired in the chapter at the session-70 adversarial review: the lore row now reads thirteen firing outright, the other twelve silent, and a thirteenth, compactness (P26), lending only its flavor; thirteen firing, one flavor-only, twelve silent is and was the honest count. Dated, session 83 (the registry-residuals sweep, same rules, machine-side): the chapters shipped since the session-55 build are now swept and recorded — Ch. 18's ship rows (P44's home row; P10 via Ex 1's hint, its only hint-named pattern) were installed at its ship; Ch. 19's ship rows (P45's home row; the P13/P25/P10 cross-refs) at its authoring; Ch. 20's eighteen exercises carry exactly one hint-named pattern — Exercise 6's hint names divisibility as P41's cameo, P41's first hint-named problem, taking it out of the forward-registry state (the row and the P27–P42 footnote above record the dated correction); Ch. 21's eighteen exercises carry no pattern-ID hints (the Ch. 13–16 design, machine-verified at this sweep), so it contributes no rows.

---

## 4 · How sessions work (working protocol)

1. **Open this canvas first** — the session log says exactly where we stopped.
2. **Checkpoint before work.** Immediately add a session-log row marked "in progress" with the planned units — *before* any authoring.
3. **Update after every unit, not at the end.** As soon as a chapter, section, lab, or verification run completes, update the chapter map, to-dos, and session log right away. If a rate or usage limit interrupts the session, at most the single unit in progress is lost — and the log already names it.
4. **Chapter canvas first, atlas rows second** — the chapter canvas is the durable artifact; the atlas only indexes it.
5. **Never rewrite finished chapters** from scratch; extend them.
6. **Every chapter carries a Computational Lab** (see Conventions): the algorithm is executed and verified in-session, embedded as portable Python, and the recorded outputs are the actual verified results.
7. **Slow mode (adopted session 8, on the user's request).** Improving a chapter may span several sessions. When a review or plan has more items than one session safely fits, open a *chapter improvement tracker* in the standing to-do: one line per item, ☐/☑ status. A session may close with items still open — the tracker, not memory, is the resume point. New chapter rewrites wait until the active tracker closes.

---

## 5 · Conventions

- **Notation.** n = number of vertices, m = number of edges, d(v) = degree, Δ = max degree, χ = chromatic number, α = independence number, ω = clique number, C(n,k) = binomial "n choose k".
- **"Complete proof"** = every logical step present. **"Sketch"** = the architecture is given; the missing steps are named.
- **Sources of truth for statements** — classic texts to verify against when extending: van Lint & Wilson *A Course in Combinatorics*; Diestel *Graph Theory*; Alon & Spencer *The Probabilistic Method*; Bollobás *Extremal Graph Theory*; Lovász *Combinatorial Problems and Exercises*; Stanley *Enumerative Combinatorics I*; West *Introduction to Graph Theory*; Engel *Problem-Solving Strategies*.
- **Computational labs.** Every chapter carries a lab: the algorithm is executed and verified in-session (the sandbox has an operation budget, so labs use small n / few trials), embedded as portable Python 3 for the reader's machine, with the actual verified outputs recorded — never invented. The reader's own machine (e.g., a Raspberry Pi) has no budget: scale n up there.
- **Verification levels (strongest → weakest).** (1) *Executed lab* — algorithm ran in-session, outputs recorded. (2) *Complete human proof* — every logical step present. (3) *Flagged sketch* — architecture given, missing steps named. (4) *Formal track* — Lean 4 code written for the reader to compile; **never claimed verified until the reader reports a successful build** (Lean cannot run in the authoring sandbox).
- **Editorial standard — the Feynman rule.** Chapters are written as *lecture courses*: intuition and story before formalism; complete proofs inside the narrative; "how you could have found this" reconstructions; dialogue with the reader ("compute it yourself before reading on"); a compact reference card at the end. Chapter 1 is the model (session 5). Reference-edition chapters are re-issued lecture-style one per session; new chapters ship lecture-style from the start. The Lean formal track (Ch. 17) stays a single living cross-cutting companion, updated as chapters are written or rewritten — never one Lean chapter per math chapter.
- **Editorial standard — the web edition** (adopted session 21, on the user's request): every chapter ships alongside its markdown canvas a **web edition** — a standalone HTML page embedding the chapter source byte-identically (base64 payload) and rendering it client-side with the export-safe CSS (`white-space: pre`, `break-inside: avoid`, A4 `@page` print rules). Print-to-PDF from any browser is the Atlas's standard PDF route while the platform's export pipeline is broken. Feynman rewrites and new chapters get their web edition at delivery time (Ch. 1–4 have theirs). **The rebuild rule:** a chapter edit invalidates its web edition — rebuild and re-verify byte-exact on every source change; a stale embed is a defect. The markdown canvas stays the authoritative source. **Web-frame retirement (session 78, user-directed):** the per-chapter web-edition canvases are retired from the durable master copy — every frame's payload is its chapter master and the renderMd v2 engine is byte-identical (md5 d5eaa031…) across all sixteen, so only one frame survives: `cgt-ch1-web.md`, the canonical frame/engine master (the session-77 canary). A chapter edit no longer builds a web edition; it rebakes the .html export directly from the chapter master via the canonical frame (see the HTML export format convention).
- **Editorial standard — the exercise system** (adopted session 7, on the user's request): every chapter carries a graded exercise set — ●○○ easy (direct application of one lecture idea), ●●○ moderate (two ideas or one tricky step), ●●● tough (several chapter ideas combined) — with hints inline and complete worked answers at the end of the chapter, so the learner tries first and checks later. Chapters 1 and 3 are the models.
- **Editorial standard — the discovery cycle** (adopted session 8, from the external review of Ch. 1): every lecture aims to place the reader in the discoverer's seat — **Encounter** (surprising concrete problem) → **Experiment** (small cases, by hand or code) → **Conjecture** (predict before the formula) → **Struggle** (try a method, including a plausible wrong one — show the wrong turn that teaches) → **Explain** (the rigorous argument, verified) → **Generalize** (change the problem; see what survives). The sixth stage is where research begins. Discovery checkpoints (short "try it yourself before reading on" moments) are being retrofitted into existing lectures via the improvement trackers.
- **Explorations category** (adopted session 8): beyond the graded set, each chapter may carry ◐ **Explorations** — open-ended investigations with no single right answer. Each states *what you should find* (the law, the pattern, the shape of the answer) rather than a number to match. These are research-style training, not test preparation.
- **Labs test mappings, not just totals** (adopted session 8 — learned the hard way when a wrong reflection-bijection inverse survived because the totals happened to match): when a chapter claims a bijection, the lab implements the map **and its inverse** and round-trips them over *all* small inputs. Matching totals certify a count; only round-trips certify a correspondence.
- **Pattern-unknown ◆ exercises** (adopted session 10, from the second external review of Ch. 1): every chapter's exercise set ends with a few problems that name *no* method and carry no hint — the reader must diagnose which tool fits (inclusion–exclusion, bijection, recurrence, double counting…). Difficulty ratings are withheld too: sizing an unseen problem is part of the problem. Worked answers still go at the end. Ch. 1's three (subsets-without-consecutives → Fibonacci; even/odd subset involution; line-arrangement regions) are the models.
- **Small-case tests are detectors, not proofs** (adopted session 10): labs and round-trip checks certify the cases they run; general claims are settled by arguments. A chapter may never say or imply that computation replaces proof — and every lab should state what it does *not* establish.
- **Revision history lives in an appendix, not in the lectures** (adopted session 11, from the third external review of Ch. 1): chapters never interrupt the lecture body with session numbers, review stories, or correction history. Each chapter closes with a *Revision History & Verification Notes* appendix (editions, verification record, lab/rendering notes, standing decisions). Ch. 1 is the model.
- **Publishing and pipeline material lives in the publishing toolkit, not in chapter appendices** (adopted session 17, from the seventh external review of Ch. 1): chapters keep at most a one-paragraph rendering note in their revision appendix; export configuration, verification scripts, and standalone code distributions belong to the publishing toolkit canvas. Chapters are mathematics; the toolkit is plumbing.
- **Chapter numbering (session 56, user-directed).** The Lean Formal Track moved from Ch. 13 to the end as Ch. 17 (the last chapter); the four Volume III chapters shifted one step ahead — Probabilistic Act II 14 → 13, Regularity 15 → 14, Graph Structure 16 → 15, Symmetry & Design 17 → 16 — so Volume III is now Ch. 13–16. Historical session-log rows and completed-ledger lines keep the numbering of their time; every current-facing surface (chapter texts, web editions, .html exports, the chapter map, the pattern indexes, the project KNOWLEDGE) carries the new numbers. Chapter references are context-anchored tokens (Ch. N / Chapter N / chN) — never bare numbers.
- **Chapter numbering, second pass (session 80, user-directed).** The Lean Formal Track's Ch. 17 number was CANCELLED — the track is an unnumbered project idea, touched at the very end, after every numbered chapter; the two survey-proposed chapters take the freed numbers as Ch. 17 (The Semi-Random Method, P43's home) and Ch. 18 (Thresholds and Windows, P44's home) in the new Volume IV. Historical session-log rows and completed-ledger lines keep the numbering of their time (a pre-session-80 row that says Ch. 17 = the Lean track remains honest); every current-facing surface carries the new state. Chapter references stay context-anchored tokens (Ch. N / Chapter N / chN) — never bare numbers.
- **HTML export format (session 57, user-directed).** The .html exports (Ch. 1–17) are fully pre-rendered plain HTML: the chapter content is rendered at build time by the web frame's own markdown renderer (evaluated verbatim, not re-implemented) and baked into the page — no encoded payload, no script; they open identically with JavaScript disabled. The .md web editions remain the canonical byte-identical embed route (the payload decode oracle is unchanged), and the .html regeneration rule is now "decode the payload, render it, bake it into the frame" — the old `.html == web .md lines 7+` identity is retired. **Export hygiene (session 78, user-directed).** The .html exports ship clean: no export banner at the top of the body, no Revision History & Verification Notes appendix, and no chapter-status footer at the end — that development record lives in the chapter canvas only. The standing rebake route from now on: chapter master → render via the canonical frame's renderMd v2 engine → bake into the head/tail shell → strip the banner, the appendix, and the status footer → verify (zero `Reference web edition`, zero `Revision History`, zero status string; line 38 = `<main id="content">`; tail = `</main></body></html>`; mojibake 0). All sixteen exports were cleaned in place at session 78 under exactly these gates (16/16 PASS).
